\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holds in
T
:

if
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
can be applied on
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
as defined in
Table

16

then
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
holds in
T_{r}
with
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{Y}
,
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}=
fact identifier

and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\emptyset
.

Minimize
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}

Complete
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}

Proposition 0 (Propagation rules for filter, project and pivot).

Let
T_{r}(S_{r})=\mathpzc{Q}\,(T)
be the result of a filter, project or pivot query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
over an analytic table
T(\textsc{S})
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
be an attribute of
T_{r}
, and
\textsc{S}_{D}
be the set of dimension attributes in
T
.
Then the propagation rules of
Table

19
for filter, project and pivot are correct.

Proof.

Suppose that whenever
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in T
then
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holds in
T
, for
\mathrm{X}\subseteq\textsc{S}_{D}
.

(1)

Filter queries: Let
T_{r}=\mathpzc{Filter}_{T}(P\mid\mathrm{Y})
. Then
T_{r}
is a subset of
T
and all conditions, and in particular the literal functional dependencies, in the
Definition

3.1
of for
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
still hold for
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
and we obtain
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}=\mathrm{X}_{d}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{f}
.

(2)

Projection queries: Let
T_{r}=\mathpzc{Project}_{T}(\mathrm{Y},f(\mathrm{Z})\rightarrow\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{M}})
.

•

\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}
: Since, by definition of projection,
\mathrm{Y}
contains all dimension attributes of
T
,
T_{r}
also contains all dimension attributes of
T
(and possibly some other measure attributes). Therefore, all conditions in
Definition

3.1
still hold for all measure and dimension attributes
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}
and we obtain
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}=\mathrm{X}_{d}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{f}
.

•

For new measure attribute

M

, we have to show that
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}
must be a determinant of

M

: Since
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}
is a determinant of
\mathrm{Z}
and

M

is the result of a function applied to attributes
\mathrm{Z}
, by transitivity,
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}
is also a determinant of

M

.

(3)

Pivot queries: Let
T_{r}=\mathpzc{Pivot}_{T}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\mid\mathrm{Y})
.

•

\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
in
\textsc{S}-\mathrm{Y}-\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}
and
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holds in
T
: If
\mathrm{X}_{d}\cap\mathrm{Y}=\emptyset
, by definition of pivot, each tuple
t\in T
is mapped to a tuple
t^{\prime}\in T_{r}
where
t.(\mathrm{X}_{d}\cup\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime})=t^{\prime}.(\mathrm{X}_{d}\cup\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime})
and we obtain
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}=\mathrm{X}_{d}
is a determinant of
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
. If
\mathrm{X}_{d}\cap\mathrm{Y}\not=\emptyset
, we cannot conclude that the remaining attribute set in
\mathrm{X}_{d}-\mathrm{Y}
is still a determinant of
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
and we must apply the default rules to find
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}
. However, observe that
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
existed in the input table and we can conclude that all remaining forbidden attributes
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{f}-\mathrm{Y}
are still forbidden.

•

\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is a new attribute that holds pivoted values of

A

and
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holds in
T
:
If
\mathrm{X}_{d}
is a determinant of

A

in
T
and
\mathrm{X}_{d}\not\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
, we can conclude that the "remaining" attributes
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}=\mathrm{X}_{d}-\mathrm{Y}
are a determinant of
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
. Suppose that there are two tuples
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
in
T_{r}
which have the same value for
\mathrm{X}_{d}-\mathrm{Y}
, but different values for attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
:
t_{1}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\neq t_{2}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
. By definition of pivot, these two tuples are the result of two distinct tuples
t^{\prime}_{1}
and
t^{\prime}_{2}
in
T
where
t_{1}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}=t^{\prime}_{1}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
,
t_{2}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}=t^{\prime}_{2}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
,
t^{\prime}_{1}.\mathrm{Y}=t^{\prime}_{2}.\mathrm{Y}
, and
t^{\prime}_{1}.(\mathrm{X}_{d}-\mathrm{Y})=t^{\prime}_{2}.(\mathrm{X}_{d}-\mathrm{Y})
. We get
t.\mathrm{X}_{d}=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{X}_{d}
and
t^{\prime}_{1}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\neq t^{\prime}_{2}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
which is in contradiction with
\mathrm{X}_{d}
is a determinant of
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
. If
\mathrm{X}_{d}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
we have to recompute the determinant set of all pivoted attributes. All attributes which were forbidden for

A

are also forbidden for
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
and we obtain
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{f}-\mathrm{Y}
.

∎

We next define the following proposition for attributes in the result of an aggregate query.

Proposition 0 (Propagation rule for aggregation).

Let
T(\textsc{S})
be an analytic table with dimension attributes
\textsc{S}_{D}\subseteq\textsc{S}
, and
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
be an aggregable property that holds in
T
with determinant
\mathrm{X}_{d}
and forbidden set
\mathrm{X}_{f}
.
Let
T_{r}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Y})
be a valid aggregate query (i.e.,
\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
).
Then the propagation rule of
Table

19
for aggregation is correct.

Proof.

We prove each case of attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
:

(1)

Every attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}
is a dimension attribute in
T
and
T_{r}
. For every aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
that holds in
T
, we must only determine
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}
for
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
in
T_{r}
. Since
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is aggregable along
\mathrm{X}
, it is also aggregable along the subset of remaining attributes
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}\cap\mathrm{Y}
and all remaining forbidden attributes
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{f}\cap\mathrm{Y}
are still forbidden. We conclude that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
holds in
T_{r}
where
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}\cap\mathrm{Y}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{f}\cap\mathrm{Y}
.

(2)

We show that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
holds for new attribute
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})
in
T_{r}
with
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}_{d}^{{}^{\prime}+}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{Y}
. By the assumption in
Item

1
of
Definition

3.1
,
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
is applicable to
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})
.
By applying the default rules of
Table

17

\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}
is the fact identifier of
T_{r}
and determines
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})
as well as all attributes in
\mathrm{Y}
(
\mathrm{Y}=\mathrm{X}_{d}^{{}^{\prime}+}
is the closure of
\mathrm{X}_{d}
).
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}
is by default empty.
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}
and must be validated by the user by removing incorrect attributes from
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}
and adding meaningless attributes to
\mathrm{X}_{f}
.

∎

.

Proposition 0 (propagation rule for merge).

Let
T(S)
and
T^{\prime}(S^{\prime})
be two analytic tables with dimension attributes
S_{D}\subseteq S
and
S^{\prime}_{D}\subseteq S^{\prime}
respectively.
Let
T_{r}(S_{r})
be the result of a merge query between
T
and
T^{\prime}
over a set of common dimension attributes
\mathrm{Y}\subseteq S_{D}\cap S^{\prime}_{D}
and let
\textsc{S}^{r}_{D}=S_{D}\cup S^{\prime}_{D}
be the dimension attributes in
T_{r}
.
Let
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in S_{r}
be an attribute of
T
with aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holding in
T
.

Then the propagation rules of
Table

21
for merge queries are correct.

Proof.

We proceed with each case of attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
:

(1)

If
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is a dimension attribute, by
Definition

3.1
,

\mathrm{X}=S_{D}-\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}-\mathrm{X}_{f}
and since
\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}\cup\mathrm{X}_{f}\subseteq S_{D}
(all forbidden attributes are in
S_{D}
),
we can add all attributes of
S^{\prime}_{D}
which are not in
S_{D}
to
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
:
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}\cup(S^{\prime}_{D}-\mathrm{Y})
.
The user must add all new meaningless attributes in
S^{\prime}_{D}
to
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{f}
.

(2)

If
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is a measure attribute,

\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is an attribute of table
T
but not of table
T^{\prime}
. Let
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{X}_{d}^{+}-\mathrm{X}_{f}
, as in
Definition

3.1
.
We show that the LFD
\mathrm{X}_{d}\mapsto\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is still valid in
T_{r}
. Suppose that there exist two tuples
t
and
t^{\prime}
in
T_{r}
such that
t.X_{d}\equiv t^{\prime}.X_{d}
and
t.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\not\equiv t^{\prime}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
.
We show that, for each merge operation, the projection of these two tuples on
S
would also exist in
T
, which contradicts that LFD
\mathrm{X}_{d}\mapsto\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
holds in
T
:

•

If
T_{r}=T\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}
(left merge):
by definition of
\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\mathrm{Y}}
any projection on
S
of a tuple
T
in
T_{r}
is also a tuple in
T
(similar to filter queries).

•

If
T_{r}=T\mathbin{\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}
(right merge):
any projection on
S
of a tuple
T
in
T_{r}
is either a tuple in
T
or a tuple that does not exist in
T
and has a null value for each attribute in
S-\mathrm{Y}
. In the latter case, the LFD is preserved in
T_{r}
because all these tuples also have a null value on

A

and there is no tuple in
T
that has null values on
\mathrm{X}_{d}
and a non-null value for

A

.

•

If
T_{r}=T\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}
(full merge): The proof as a combination of the proofs for left merge and right merge (any projection on
S
of a tuple
T
in
T_{r}
is either a tuple in
T
or a tuple that does not exist in
T
and has a null value for each attribute in
S-\mathrm{Y}
).

•

If
T_{r}=T\bowtie_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}
(strict merge):
any projection on
S
of a tuple
T
in
T_{r}
is also a tuple in
T
.

Then
\mathrm{X}_{d}
is still a minimum set of dimension attributes in
T_{r}
which literally determines
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
(otherwise it would not be minimum in
T
) and, by
Definition

3.1
,
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
contains all attributes determined by
\mathrm{X}_{d}
in
T_{r}
(closure of
\mathrm{X}_{d}
in
T_{r}
). Similarly, all meaningless attributes in
\mathrm{X}_{f}
remain meaningless and the user can add new meaningless attributes from
S^{\prime}-\mathrm{Y}
.

∎

In
Table

21
, in the case of a merge query,
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{f}
by default
and for a measure attribute, the attribute graphs of each dimension are used to compute the closure
\mathrm{X}_{d}^{+}
over
S^{\prime}_{D}
.
In the propagation rule, we only considered the case of an attribute

A

in
T
, but the same result would apply for an attribute

A

in
T^{\prime}
due to the symmetry of the merge operations.

Example 0.

In
Table

22
,
table
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_YEARLY}}
is defined over dimensions
{SALESORG}
and
{TIME}
, table
\mathrm{{DEM2}}
is defined over
{REGION}
(see
Table

1(b)
) and
{TIME}
, and
table
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_DEM}}
is the result of the left-merge query:

\displaystyle\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_DEM}}=\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_YEARLY}}\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{CITY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}}\mathrm{{DEM2}}

We first consider attribute

AMOUNT

. Suppose that
\mathbf{agg}_{AMOUNT}(\mathrm{SUM},\mathrm{X})
holds in
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_YEARLY}}
for
\mathrm{X}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{STORE\_ID}},\allowbreak\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{CITY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
, with
\mathrm{X}_{d}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{STORE\_ID}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}

and
\mathrm{X}_{f}=\emptyset
.
Then,

\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{CITY}},\allowbreak\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{STATE}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}\}
is the set of attributes determined by
\mathrm{X}_{d}
in
\mathrm{{DEM2}}
.
Then,
\mathrm{X}^{{}^{\prime}+}_{d}=\mathrm{X}^{+}_{d}\cup\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{STORE\_ID}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{CITY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{STATE}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}\}
is the set of attributes determined by
\mathrm{X}_{d}
in
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_DEM}}
.
So, by the propagation rule of
Table

21
,
aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{AMOUNT}}}(\mathrm{SUM},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
holds in
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_DEM}}
where
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}_{d}^{{}^{\prime}+}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{d}^{{}^{\prime}+}
.

11.9

Observe that the aggregable property computed by the propagation rule for merge queries does not guarantee that any aggregation query of

AMOUNT

produces the same result when it is applied on
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_YEARLY}}
or on
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_DEM}}
. We will show in
Section

4.2
how to refine the propagation rule to guarantee this summarizability property.

We now consider attribute

UNEMP

in
\mathrm{{DEM2}}
of category
\mathbf{{STAT}}
. Suppose that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{UNEMP}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holds in
\mathrm{{DEM2}}
for
\mathrm{X}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}},\allowbreak\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{CITY}},\allowbreak\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{STATE}},\allowbreak\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}\})
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{COUNT},\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT},\mathrm{MIN},\mathrm{MAX}\}
with
\mathrm{X}_{d}=X
.
We use the propagation rule of
Table

21
on the (equivalent) right-merge of
\mathrm{{DEM2}}
with
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_YEARLY}}
. No new attribute of
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_YEARLY}}
is determined by
\mathrm{X}_{d}
, so
\mathrm{X}_{d}^{{}^{\prime}+}=\mathrm{X}_{d}
. Hence, by the propagation rule,
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{UNEMP}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
holds in
\mathrm{{STORE\_SALES\_DEM}}
for
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}_{d}^{{}^{\prime}+}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{f}=\mathrm{X}_{d}=\mathrm{X}
.

The next proposition states which aggregable properties hold for attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
in the union
T_{r}=T\cup T^{\prime}
and the set difference
T_{r}=T-T^{\prime}
, knowing the aggregable properties of
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
in tables
T
and
T^{\prime}
.

Proposition 0 (propagation rules for union and difference).

Let

T(S)
and
T^{\prime}(S)
be two tables over the same schema
S
having a set of dimension attributes
\mathrm{X}
. Let
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{$\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}$'}}\in S
be an attribute with aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})

holding in both tables
T
and
T^{\prime}
.
Then the propagation rules of
Table

21
for set union and difference are correct.

Proof.

We distinguish each case where
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is a dimension attribute or a measure attribute in
T_{r}(S_{r})
:

(1)

If
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is a dimension attribute: let
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\textsc{S}_{D}-\{A\}-\mathrm{X}_{f}
, where
\textsc{S}_{D}
is the set of dimension attributes in
S
. Since
S_{r}=\textsc{S}
for difference and union, and
\mathrm{X}_{f}
is defined for a given set of attributes (independently of a specific table),
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
does not change in the aggregable property of
T_{r}
for
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is a measure attribute: let
\mathrm{Z}=\mathrm{X}_{d}^{+}-\mathrm{X}_{f}
. Then
\mathrm{X}_{d}\mapsto\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
in each table
T
and
T^{\prime}
. We analyze each query case:

•

Difference: By definition of analytic difference queries,
T_{r}\subseteq T
, thus
\mathrm{X}_{d}\mapsto\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
also holds in
T_{r}=T-T^{\prime}
.

•

Union:
there could be two tuples that have the same values on their
\mathrm{X}
attributes, so we must check that
\mathrm{X}_{d}\mapsto A
holds in
T_{r}=T\cup T^{\prime}
. Otherwise, we apply the default rules for initializing
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{d}
.

Finally,
\mathrm{X}_{f}
must be the same in
T_{r}
since it only depends on the schema.

∎

Propagation rules for all operations, except union, are immediate to compute because they only involve the manipulation of metadata properties. In the case of union, when a measure depends on a subset of the dimensions of a fact table, the propagation rule requires a uniqueness test (to check the LFD
\mathrm{X}_{d}\mapsto A
) on the result of the union. Since the uniqueness test involves hierarchical dimension attributes only, it can be performed efficiently using specific data structures used to represent fact tables in main-memory (see
(
Brunel et al. 2015
;
Brunel
et al. 2016
)
).

4.
Summarizability of aggregable attributes

In this section, we consider the properties of attributes that characterize the equivalence between computing an aggregated value of an attribute from a table
T
and computing the same aggregated value from the result of a query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
over
T
.
In
Section

4.1
, we first define the property of
summarizable attributes
in the case when
\mathpzc{Q}\,
is an aggregate query, which corresponds to the traditional notion of summarizability addressed by previous work. In
Section

4.2
, we then extend our propagation rules to compute aggregable properties such that aggregate queries can only be expressed over summarizable attributes. Finally, in
Section

4.3
, we introduce the new property of
G-summarizability
of attributes in the case when
\mathpzc{Q}\,
is any analytic query. In
Section

4.4
, we then again extend our propagation rules to compute aggregable properties such that aggregation queries are expressed over G-summarizable attributes only.

4.1.
Summarizable attributes and distributive functions

Figure

9
illustrates the definition of summarizable attributes.
It depicts that when some attribute

A

of table
T
is aggregated with some function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
for each partition of attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
, it is possible to obtain the same result, by first aggregating

A

for each partition of attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
where
Z_{2}\subset Z_{1}
, using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
, and then further aggregating

A

for each partition
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
, using either the same function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
or a different function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
. We shall say that

A

is summarizable with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
, using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
.

Figure 9
.
Summarizable attribute
A
with respect to
Z_{1}
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
using
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}

The
Definition

4.1
below formalizes
Figure

9
and, as shown later in
Section

5
, subsumes the definition of summarizability addressed in previous work.
For simplicity, we use the expression
valid aggregate query
for a query
\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z})
such that there exists an aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
that holds in
T
, and
Z
contains the dimension attributes of
T
that are not in
\mathrm{X}
.

Definition 0 (Summarizable attribute).

Let
T(\textsc{S})
be an analytic table,

A

be an aggregable attribute of
T
, and

T_{1}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{1})
be a valid aggregate query over
T
.
If for
any
subset
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Z}_{1}
, there exists an applicable aggregate function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
such that the equation

(1)

\displaystyle\mathpzc{Agg}_{T_{1}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}))\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})

holds, then

A

is said to be
summarizable in
T
with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
.

Attribute summarizability is related to the notion of distributive aggregation functions, also called decomposable aggregation functions in
(
Jesus
et al. 2011
)
.

Definition 0 (Distributive aggregation function).

Let
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
be an aggregation function applicable to a set of domain values
\mathrm{V}
and
P=\{\mathrm{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{V}_{n}\}
,
n\geq 1
be a partitioning of
\mathrm{V}
.
If there exists an aggregate function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
such that

\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{V}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\mathrm{V}_{n})=\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{V}_{1})\cup\ldots\cup\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{V}_{n}))
then
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is said to be
distributive on partitioning
P
using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
.

If
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
on any partitioning of domain
\mathrm{V}
, we say that
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
over domain
\mathrm{V}
.
If
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
over any domain
\mathrm{V}
, we say that
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
, and if
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}=\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
, we simply say that
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive.

It is easy to show that functions
\mathrm{SUM},\mathrm{MIN}
and
\mathrm{MAX}
are distributive, function
\mathrm{COUNT}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{SUM}
whereas function
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}

is distributive using function
\mathrm{SUM}
only on partitionings where the same value does not appear in two distinct partitions. Finally, function
\mathrm{AVG}
is distributive over all domains
V
containing only two elements or where all elements are identical.

Example 0.

For
V=\{1,2,2,3\}
we have
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{V})=3
. Then
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
is distributive using
\mathrm{SUM}
on partitioning
P^{\prime}=\{\{1,2,2\},\{3\}\}
:

\mathrm{SUM}(\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\{1,2,2\}),\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}\{3\}))=3

However,
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
is not distributive using
\mathrm{SUM}
on partitioning
P=\{\{1,2\},\{2,3\}\}
:

\mathrm{SUM}(\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\{1,2\}),\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\{2,3\})=\mathrm{SUM}(2,3)=4

We say that
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is
distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
on attribute

A

of table
T
with partitioning attributes
\mathrm{Z}
if
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
on all partitions of the values of

A

in
T
defined by
\mathrm{Z}
and any subset of
\mathrm{Z}
.
The following proposition relates the definition of distributive functions to the notion of summarizable attributes.

Proposition 0 (Function distributivity and attribute summarizability).

Let
T(\textsc{S})
be an analytic table with dimension attributes
\textsc{S}_{D}\subseteq\textsc{S}
and an aggregable attribute

A

such that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holds in
T
.
If
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
on attribute

A

in table
T
with partitioning attributes
\mathrm{Z}\supseteq\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}

then

A

is summarizable with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holds in
T
and
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{1})
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is
distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
on attribute

A

of table
T
with partitioning attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
. To prove that

A

is summarizable in
T
with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
, we prove that for any subset
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Z}_{1}
, the following equation holds:

(2)

\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T_{1}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}))\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})

First, it is obvious that both tables
T
and
T_{1}
contain the same
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
values and therefore, the result tables in
Eq.

2
contain the same tuples with distinct
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
values.
We now show that for each pair of tuples
t\in\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})
and
t^{\prime}\in\mathpzc{Agg}_{T_{1}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}))\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})
where
t.\mathrm{Z}_{2}=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{Z}_{2}
, we have
t.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}))
. Let
x=t.\mathrm{Z}_{2}=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{Z}_{2}
and
T^{x}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}_{2}=t.\mathrm{Z}_{2}}(T)
and
T_{1}^{x}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}_{2}=t.\mathrm{Z}_{2}}(T_{1})
be the partitions of
T
and
T_{1}
on attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
corresponding to the tuples used to compute
t.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})
. For each tuple
t^{\prime}_{i}\in T_{1}^{x}
there also exists a partition
T^{y_{i}}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}_{1}=y_{i}}(T)
of
T
where
y_{i}=t^{\prime}_{i}.\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and
t_{i}^{\prime}.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})=\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\pi_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T^{y_{i}}))
.
All tuples
t^{\prime}_{i}
have the same
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
value
x=t.\mathrm{Z}_{2}
and are aggregated to tuple
t^{\prime}
whose value for attribute
t^{\prime}.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}))=\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\pi_{A}(T^{y_{1}})\cup\ldots\cup\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\pi_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T^{y_{n}})
. Since
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
and
T^{x}=T^{y_{1}}\cup\ldots\cup T^{y_{n}}
, we obtain
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\pi_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T^{y_{1}})\cup\ldots\cup\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\pi_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T^{y_{n}}))=\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\pi_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T^{y_{1}})\cup\ldots\cup\pi_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T^{y_{n}}))=\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\pi_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T^{x}))
. We conclude
t.\mathrm{Z}_{2}=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{Z}_{2}
and
t.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}))
.
∎

Example 0.

Function
\mathrm{COUNT}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{SUM}
. Therefore,

PROD_SKU

in table
\mathrm{{PRODUCT\_LIST}}
(
Table

15
on
Table

15
) is summarizable with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
and
\mathrm{COUNT}
using function
\mathrm{SUM}
. Thus, if
\mathrm{Z}_{2}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
and
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{PRODUCT\_LIST}(\mathrm{COUNT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})\mid\mathrm{Z})
, the following equation folds:

\mathpzc{Agg}_{PRODUCT\_LIST}(\mathrm{COUNT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T_{1}}(\mathrm{SUM}(\mathrm{COUNT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}}))\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})

However, as explained before,
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
is only distributive using
\mathrm{SUM}
if no pair of partitions share the same value. This is not the case (there exist two partitions of
Z
with the same product ”cz-tshirt-s”), so attribute

PROD_SKU

is not summarizable with respect to grouping set
Z
and function
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
.

Function distributivity is a sufficient but not a necessary condition for summarizability. This is illustrated in the following proposition, which defines a sufficient condition for summarizability with
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
and
\mathrm{SUM}
.

Proposition 0 (Summarizability with
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
and
\mathrm{SUM}
).

Let
T(\textsc{S})
be an analytic table with a set of dimension attributes
\textsc{S}_{{D}}
and an aggregable attribute

A

.
Let
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{1})
be a valid aggregate query over
T
, where
\mathrm{Z}_{1}\subseteq\textsc{S}_{{D}}
.
If
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and the literal functional dependency
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}\mapsto\mathrm{Z}_{1}
holds in
T
, the following equation is true:

(3)

\mathpzc{Agg}_{T_{1}}(\mathrm{SUM}(\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}))\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})

We say that attribute

A

(in
T
) is summarizable with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
using function
\mathrm{SUM}

with partitioning attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
.

Proof.

The previous proposition mainly states that

A

is summarizable with respect to
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
using function
\mathrm{SUM}
with partitioning attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
if all tuples
T
in some partition
T^{x}\subseteq T
defined by attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}_{1}
which have the same value for attribute
t.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}
are assigned to the
same partition

T^{y}\subseteq T
defined by attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
. This avoids the double counting of distinct

A

values when taking the
\mathrm{SUM}
of
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
over the partitions generated by attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
.

We first show by contradiction that when
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}\mapsto\mathrm{Z}_{1}-\mathrm{Z}_{2}
holds in
T
, all tuples
T
in some partition
T^{x}\subseteq T
of
T
generated by attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
with the same value for attribute
t.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}
are assigned to the same partition
T^{y}\subseteq T
generated by attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
.

Let
T^{x}
be a partition of
T
which contains all tuples
T
such that
t.\mathrm{Z}_{2}=x
.
Since
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Z}_{1}
,
T^{x}
is the union of a set of partitions
T^{y}_{0}\ldots,T^{y}_{n}
,
n\geq 0
of
T
defined by attributes
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
.
Suppose that there exist two tuples
t\in T^{y}_{i}
and
t^{\prime}\in T^{y}_{j}
where
i\neq j
and
t.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}
. Then, we have
t.\mathrm{Z}_{2}=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{Z}_{2}=x
,
t.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}=t^{\prime}.\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}
and, since
i\neq j
,
t.\mathrm{Z}_{1}\neq t^{\prime}.\mathrm{Z}_{1}
(two different partitions generated by
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
contain the same values for
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
and

A

). This is in contradiction with
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}\mapsto\mathrm{Z}_{1}-\mathrm{Z}_{2}
. Then, if
d_{i}
is the number of distinct

A

values in some partition
T^{x}_{i}\subseteq T
, we can easily show that
\sum_{i=0}^{n}d_{i}
is the number of distinct

A

values in partition
V
.
∎

Observe that if
Eq.

3
holds for any subset
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Z}_{1}
,

A

is summarizable with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
using function
\mathrm{SUM}
(
Definition

4.1
).

Example 0.

Let
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{\mathrm{{PRODUCT\_LIST}}}(\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{1})
where
\mathrm{Z}_{1}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}\}
. Attribute

PROD_SKU

is not summarizable with respect to grouping set
Z_{1}
and function
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
using
\mathrm{SUM}
. However, if
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}}\mapsto\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}
holds in table
\mathrm{{PRODUCT\_LIST}}
, then for
\mathrm{Z}_{2}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}}\}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}=\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}}
, we have
\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}}\}\mapsto\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}\}
. Therefore,

PROD_SKU

is summarizable with respect to
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
and
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
using function
\mathrm{SUM}
with partitioning attribute
\mathrm{Z}_{2}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}}\}
.

4.2.
Controlling attribute summarizability using aggregable properties

Given the result of an aggregate query
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{1})
over some attribute

A

,
we want to control the possible aggregations of attribute
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})
depending on the summarizability of

A

. We use aggregable properties for that purpose, as shown on
Figure

10
. We want to automatically compute the subset of dimension attributes
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}_{1}
of
T_{1}
such that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
holds in
T_{1}
,
for some function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
that is applicable to
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})
,
and which guarantees the summarizability of

A

in
T_{1}
for any
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
such that

\mathrm{Z}_{1}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}_{2}
.
Our rationale is therefore to
refine the propagation rules introduced in
Section

3.3
to take into account the summarizability correctness criteria.

Figure 10
.
Aggregable property that controls the summarizability of
A
in
T
with respect to grouping set
Z_{1}

We next define the notion of summarizability preserving aggregable property which formalizes
Figure

10
.

Definition 0 (Summarizability preserving aggregable property).

Let
T(\textsc{S})
be an analytic table and

T_{1}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{1})
be the result of a valid aggregate query. We say that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})

preserves the summarizability of

A

with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}_{1}

if for any subset
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
such that
\mathrm{Z}_{1}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}_{2}
, attribute

A

is summarizable in
T
with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
using
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
.

The next proposition uses
Proposition

4.4
and
Proposition

4.6
to refine the previous propagation rule for aggregation in
Table

19
so that summarizability preserving aggregable properties are inferred. The refined rule for the case when
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}=\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(A)
is displayed in
Table

23
.

Proposition 0 (Propagation of aggregable properties with summarizability preservation).

Let
T(\textsc{S})
be an analytic table with dimension attributes
\textsc{S}_{D}\subseteq\textsc{S}
and let
T_{r}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Y})
be the result of a valid aggregate query.
Then the aggregable properties inferred by the rule of
Table

23
when
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}=F(A)
preserve the summarizability of

A

with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Y}
.

Proof.

By
Definition

4.8
, we have to show that for any subset
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
such that
\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}_{2}
, attribute

A

is summarizable in
T
with respect to
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
using
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
. We examine both cases of

Proposition

4.9
:

•

\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
can be applied on
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
as defined in
Table

16
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
:
Since
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
is distributive using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
, it is also distributive on attribute

A

with partitioning attributes
\mathrm{Y}
. Then, by
Proposition

4.4
,

A

is summarizable with respect to
\mathrm{Y}
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
using function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
, and
Equation

1
in
Definition

4.1
holds for any subset
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
.

•

\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}=\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
is a
maximal
subset of
\mathrm{X}\cap\mathrm{Y}
such that
(\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime})\cup\{A\}\mapsto\mathrm{Y}
holds in
T
:
By
Proposition

4.6
, it is sufficient to show that
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}\mapsto\mathrm{Y}
for all
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
where
\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}_{2}\subset\mathrm{X}\cap\mathrm{Y}
. Since
(\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime})\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}\mapsto\mathrm{Y}
and
\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}_{2}
we also have
\mathrm{Z}_{2}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}\mapsto\mathrm{Y}
.

∎

For the second condition in
Table

23
, observe that there might exist several maximal subsets of attributes
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{i}
. The process to compute these subsets is quite simple. Each maximal subset
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{i}
corresponds to a
minimal subset
of attributes
K_{i}=\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{i}\subset\mathrm{Y}
such that
K_{i}\cup\{A\}
determines all attributes of
\mathrm{Y}
. These sets
\mathrm{K}_{i}
can easily be computed using the attribute graphs of the corresponding dimensions to obtain
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{i}=(\mathrm{Y}\cap\mathrm{X})-\mathrm{K}_{i}
.

Example 0.

Aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}}}(\mathrm{COUNT}\mid\mathrm{X})
holds in table
\mathrm{{PRODUCT\_LIST}}
(
Table

8(e)
)
for
\mathrm{X}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
.
Let
T_{r}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{\mathrm{{PRODUCT\_LIST}}}(\mathrm{COUNT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})\mid\mathrm{Y})
, where
\mathrm{Y}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
.

By the first condition in
Table

23
and distributivity of
\mathrm{COUNT}
using
\mathrm{SUM}
, the aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{COUNT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})}(\mathrm{SUM}\mid\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
, where
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}\cap\mathrm{Y}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
, preserves the summarizability of

PROD_SKU

with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Y}
.

Example 0.

Property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}}}(\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}\mid\mathrm{X})
holds in table
\mathrm{{PRODUCT\_LIST}}
for
\mathrm{X}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\allowbreak\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
.
Let
T_{r}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{\mathrm{{PRODUCT\_LIST}}}(\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})\mid\mathrm{Y})
where
\mathrm{Y}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\allowbreak\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
.
By the second condition in
Table

23
, we must compute the maximum subset
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
of
\mathrm{X}\cap\mathrm{Y}
such that

(\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime})\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}\mapsto\mathrm{Y}
. We use the method explained before.
By the attribute graphs of dimension
{TIME}
and
{PROD}
, the only LFD which holds among the attributes of
\mathrm{Y}
is
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}}\mapsto\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}
.
Thus, there is a single minimal set
K=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{BRAND}},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{YEAR}}\}
such that
K\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}}\}
determines all attributes in
\mathrm{Y}
.
We obtain that
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{Y}-K=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}\}
. Hence,

\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})}(\mathrm{SUM}\mid\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}\})

preserves the summarizability of

PROD_SKU

with respect to grouping set
\mathrm{Y}
.

Aggregable properties provide "explanations" for end users of which aggregate queries preserve the summarizability condition of the aggregated attribute in a given stage of the data analysis session. In the previous example, the aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})}(\mathrm{SUM}\mid\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{COUNTRY}}\})
of
T_{r}
explains that
table
T_{r}
can be used to count the number of distinct products per brand
and
year by taking the sum of
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{PROD\_SKU}})
along

COUNTRY

. However,
T_{r}
cannot be used to obtain the number of distinct products by brand
or
by year. In this case, the user must "backtrack" in the interactive data analysis session to the table
\mathrm{{PRODUCT\_LIST}}
to obtain this number.

4.3.
Generalized attribute summarizability

In the previous sections, as illustrated in
Figure

9
, we defined an attribute

A

in some table
T
to be summarizable with respect to some aggregate query
\mathpzc{Q}\,(T)=\mathpzc{Agg}_{\mathrm{{T}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{1})
and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
using
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}
, if for any query
\mathpzc{Q}\,^{\prime}(T)=\mathpzc{Agg}_{\mathrm{{T}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})
aggregating

A

along a subset
\mathrm{Z}_{2}
of
\mathrm{Z}_{1}
, there exists an
equivalent
aggregate query
\mathpzc{Q}\,^{\prime\prime}(T_{1})=\mathpzc{Agg}_{\mathrm{{T_{1}}}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}))\mid\mathrm{Z}_{2})
on the result
T_{1}
of
\mathpzc{Q}\,(T)
.
Definition

4.12
generalizes this notion to any analytic query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
as follows.

Definition 0 (Generalized summarizable attribute).

Let
T(\textsc{S})
be an analytic table that is the input of an analytic query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
returning a table
T_{1}(\textsc{S}_{1})
.
Let

A

be an aggregable attribute of both
T
and
T_{1}
and
\mathrm{Z}
be a subset of the dimension attributes of
\textsc{S}\cap\textsc{S}_{1}
.
If for any two valid aggregate queries
\mathpzc{Q}\,^{\prime}(T)=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}^{\prime})
and
\mathpzc{Q}\,^{\prime}(T_{1})=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T_{1}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Z}^{\prime})

such that
\mathrm{Z}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}
, and any two tuples
t_{1}\in\mathpzc{Q}\,^{\prime}(T)
and
t_{2}\in\mathpzc{Q}\,^{\prime}(T_{1})
, we have:

t_{1}.\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}\equiv t_{2}.\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}\Rightarrow t_{1}.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\equiv t_{2}.\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})

then

A

is said to be
G-summarizable
in
T
with respect to query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
.

Figure 11
.
G-summarizable attribute
A
in
T
with respect to
Q
, grouping set
Z
and function
F

The above definition is illustrated in
Figure

11
.
We make a few observations. First,
T_{2}
and
T^{\prime}_{2}
are not necessarily equal,
i.e
.

T_{2}
might contain tuples that are not in
T^{\prime}_{2}
and vice versa. Second, in
Section

3.3
, we established the propagation rules to compute the aggregable properties on

A

that hold in
T_{1}
for
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
, which are used in the definition to determine which aggregate queries
\mathpzc{Q}\,^{\prime}
on
T_{1}
are valid.
Third, an implicit assumption is that

A

is an attribute that exists in both
T
and
T_{1}
. Therefore, in the case of an aggregate or a pivot query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
,

A

must be a dimension attribute (since measure attributes of
T
do not exist anymore in
T_{1}
- they have either been aggregated, pivoted or eliminated). Finally, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
defines a set of attributes that must be contained in
\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}
and implicitly restricts the attributes along which aggregation can be done in
\mathpzc{Q}\,^{\prime}
.

Before formalizing sufficient conditions for G-summarizability, we present a few examples.

Example 0.

Consider a fact table
T
(in
Table

24
) defined over two dimension
{D}_{1}
and
{D}_{2}
with dimension attributes
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{1},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{2},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}
from dimension
{D}_{1}
(where
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{1}\preccurlyeq A_{2}\preccurlyeq A_{3}
) and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{B}}_{1},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{B}}_{2}
from dimension
{D}_{2}
(where
B_{1}\preccurlyeq B_{2}
).
We shall say that within table
T
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}
is the
highest
attribute of
{D}_{1}
while
B_{2}
is the
highest
attribute of
D_{2}
.

\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}(T)=\mathpzc{Filter}_{T}(\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}=c_{1}\}\mid\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}\})
whose result table

is displayed in

.
If we take a grouping set
\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}\}
, then for each partition
T^{p}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}=p}(T)
of
T
we either have
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}(T^{p})=T^{x}
or
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}(T^{p})=\emptyset
. In our example, we have:

\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}(T^{c_{1}})=T^{c_{1}}
and
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}(T^{c_{2}})=\emptyset
. Thus,
for any subset
\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}
of dimension attributes of
T
containing
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}
, we
either have
\Pi_{\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}}(\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}(T^{p}))=\Pi_{\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}}(T^{p})
or
\Pi_{\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}}(\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}(T^{p}))
is empty,
where
\Pi
is a projection without duplicate elimination.
Therefore, any valid aggregation query with grouping attributes
\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}
containing
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}
returns, for each partition of
T_{1}
defined by
\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}
, the same result as for the corresponding partition of
T
defined by
\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}
.
Hence, any attribute

A

is G-summarizable in
T
with respect to query
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}
, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}=\{A_{3}\}
and any function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
applicable to

A

in
T
.
Observe that this is not the case for any other grouping set
Z
that does not contain attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{3}
. For instance, if
\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{2}\}
then for partition
T^{b_{1}}
,
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{1}(T^{b_{1}})\neq T^{b_{1}}
.

However, the previous reasoning does not apply if
T
is filtered on a measure attribute

M

, like in query
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{2}(T)=\mathpzc{Filter}_{T}(\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{M}}\in\{x_{3},x_{4}\}\}\mid\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{M}}\})
. Since a measure attribute cannot belong to the grouping set of an aggregate query, we cannot define a partitioning set
\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{M}}\}
.
Then, to identify a set of dimension attributes
\mathrm{Z}
such that all attributes

A

are G-summarizable in
T
with respect to query
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{2}
, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
and an applicable function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
, we must find a set of dimension attributes
\mathrm{Z}
that partitions
T
into
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{2}(T)
and
T-\mathpzc{Q}\,_{2}(T)
. In our example,
\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{B}}_{1}\}
would be a possible solution that cannot be easily found.

Example 0.

Consider now the fact tables
T
and
T^{\prime}
defined over the same dimensions
{D}_{1}
and
D_{2}
as before, as shown below in
Table

25
.
Take the left-merge query
\mathpzc{Q}\,(T,T^{\prime})=T\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}=T_{1}
where
\mathrm{Y}=\{A_{1},A_{2},B_{1},B_{2}\}
. Any attribute

A

of
T

is G-summarizable in
T
with respect to
\mathpzc{Q}\,
, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}=\emptyset
, and any function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
applicable to

A

, because the duplicate preserving projection of
T_{1}
on the attributes of
T
is equal to table
T
.

Let us look at the G-summarizability of attributes in
T^{\prime}
with respect to
\mathpzc{Q}\,
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
. First, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{2},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{B}}_{2}\}
containing the "highest" attributes in
\mathrm{Y}
defines two partitions of
T^{\prime}
.
We can see that partition
T^{\prime b_{1},e_{1}}
is different from the duplicate-preserving projection of
T_{1}^{b_{1},e_{1}}
on the attributes of
T^{\prime}
(tuples
t_{3}
,
t_{4}
of
T^{\prime}
have no corresponding tuples in
T_{1}
). So, any valid aggregation query over a partitioning on
Z
would violate the G-summarizability property.
Indeed, the only grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
for which we have the equality of non-empty partitions is
\mathrm{Z}=\{A_{1},A_{2},B_{1},B_{2}\}
.
We shall see later that if a valid aggregation can be expressed over
Y
in
T^{\prime}
then
Z
can be equal to
Y
.

Now take the same left-merge query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
as before applied to the tables displayed in
Table

26
.
For grouping set
\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{2},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{B}}_{2}\}
, the partition of
T^{\prime}
with values
(b_{1},e_{1})
has a corresponding identical partition in
T_{1}
after a duplicate-preserving projection on the attributes of
T^{\prime}
. The partition of
T^{\prime}
with values
(b_{1},e_{3})
has a corresponding empty partition in
T_{1}
. However, the partition of
T_{1}
with values
(b_{2},e_{1})
has one extra tuple with respect to the corresponding partition in
T^{\prime}
because tuple
t_{7}
is matched by two tuples of
T
and its attribute values appear duplicated in
T_{1}
(in tuples
t_{12}
and
t_{13}
).
Hence, for attributes of
T^{\prime}
, G-summarizability in
T^{\prime}
with respect to
Q
and grouping set
\mathrm{Z}=\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}_{2},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{B}}_{2}\}
must be restricted to functions that are insensitive to duplicates (i.e.,
\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}
,
\mathrm{MIN}
,
\mathrm{MAX}
).

Finally, any attribute of
T
is G-summarizable in
T
with respect to
\mathpzc{Q}\,
and grouping set
\mathrm{Z}=\emptyset
because the duplicate preserving projection of
T_{1}
on the attributes of
T
is equal to
T
. Thus,
T_{1}
is aggregable over any partitioning of attributes of
T
.

Proposition 0 (Queries satisfying G-summarizability).

Let
\mathpzc{Q}\,
be a unary or binary analytic query with some input table
T(S)
returning a table
T_{1}(\textsc{S}_{1})
and
S_{D}
be the dimension attributes in
S\cap\textsc{S}_{1}
.
Let
\mathrm{Z}
be a subset of
S_{D}
, and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
be an attribute in
S\cap\textsc{S}_{1}
such that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
and
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X}_{1})
hold in
T
and
T_{1}
respectively.
Then,
the attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is G-summarizable in
T
with respect to query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
, and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
in the following cases:

Unary queries:

(1)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=\mathpzc{Filter}_{T}(P\mid\mathrm{Y})
,
\mathrm{Y}\subseteq S_{D}
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in S

and
\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}
.

(2)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=\mathpzc{Project}_{T}(\mathrm{Y},f(\mathrm{Z}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{M}})
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}

and
\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
.

(3)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Y})
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}
,

\mathrm{Z}=\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{MIN},\allowbreak\mathrm{MAX},\allowbreak\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}\}
.

(4)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=\mathpzc{Pivot}_{T}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\mid\mathrm{Y})
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in S-\mathrm{Y}-\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}
,

\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}-\mathrm{Y}-\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\})
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{MIN},\allowbreak\mathrm{MAX},\allowbreak\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}\}
.

Merge queries:

In the following, let
\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
denote the subset of "highest" attributes in the set of join attributes
\mathrm{Y}
.

(1)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=T\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\textsc{S}
,

\mathrm{Z}=\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
and if
\mathrm{Y}\not\mapsto S_{r}
then
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{MIN},\allowbreak\mathrm{MAX},\allowbreak\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT}\}
.

(2)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=T\mathbin{\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}
or
\mathpzc{Q}\,=T\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}
:

(a)

If
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}
and for all non-empty partitions
T^{\prime y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime})
of
T^{\prime}
, the corresponding partition
T^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T)
of
T
is empty or
\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(T^{y})
is
equal
to
\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(T^{\prime y})
, then
\mathrm{Z}=\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}

(b)

If
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\textsc{S}-\mathrm{Y}
and for all non-empty partitions
T^{\prime y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Y}=y}(T^{\prime})
of
T^{\prime}
and corresponding partitions
T^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Y}=y}(T)
of
T
,
\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(T^{y})
is a
subset of

\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(T^{\prime y})
, then
\mathrm{Z}=\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
.

(c)

Otherwise,
\mathrm{Z}=(\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1})\cup\mathrm{Y}
.

In addition, for all cases, if
\mathrm{Y}\not\mapsto S_{r}
then
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT},\mathrm{MIN},\mathrm{MAX}\}
.

(3)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=T\bowtie_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\textsc{S}
: if for all non-empty partitions
T^{\prime y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime})
and corresponding partitions
T^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T)
,
\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(T^{\prime y})
contains
\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(T^{y})
then
\mathrm{Z}=\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
else
\mathrm{Z}=\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}
.

In addition, if
\mathrm{Y}\not\mapsto S_{r}
then
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT},\mathrm{MIN},\mathrm{MAX}\}
.

Set queries:

In the following, let
\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}\subseteq S_{D}
denote the set of "highest" attributes in the set of dimension attributes
S_{D}\subseteq S
of
T
and
T^{\prime}
.

(1)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=T\cup T^{\prime}
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\textsc{S}
and if
\pi_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}}(T)\cap\pi_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}}(T^{\prime})=\emptyset
, then
\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
.

(2)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=T-T^{\prime}
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\textsc{S}
and if all partitions
\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T)
are equal to or disjoint with
\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime})
, then

\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
.

Proof.

Unary queries:

We shall use symbol
\Pi
to denote the duplicate preserving projection and
\pi
do denote duplicate eliminating projection. For each case of a unary query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
on some table
T
producing a table
T_{1}
, we first prove that, for any partition
T^{x}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}=x}(T)
of
T
and
T_{1}^{x}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}=x}(T_{1})
of
T_{1}
, we have: (1)
\pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T_{1}^{x})=\pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T^{x})
(both partitions are equal modulo duplicates), or (2)
T^{x}
is empty, or (3)
T_{1}^{x}
is empty. We call the previous condition the
G-summarizability condition on
T
and
T_{1}
for
\mathrm{Z}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
. If this condition holds, we can show that
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is G-summarizale in
T
with respect to
\mathpzc{Q}\,
, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
as follows:

•

In case (1), if
\mathrm{Z}\mapsto\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
, then
\Pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T_{1}^{x})=\Pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T^{x})
(both partitions are identical including duplicates) and it is obvious that any query, that aggregates
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
using
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
grouped by
\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}
containing all attributes in
\mathrm{Z}
, produces the same result on
T
and
T_{1}
and the conditions for G-summarizability are fulfilled. Otherwise, if
\mathrm{Z}\not\mapsto\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
,
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
must be restricted to aggregation functions that are not sensitive to duplicates (
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT},\mathrm{MIN},\mathrm{MAX}\}
).

•

In cases (2) and (3), the aggregated value does not exist, respectively, in the input table
T
or the result table
T_{1}
. This is also sufficient for satisfying the G-summarizability property.

Then, since
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X}_{1})
holds in
T_{1}
, any query that aggregates
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
using
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
grouped by
\mathrm{Z}^{\prime}
containing all dimension attributes in
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
is valid. Thus it is sufficient to show that
\mathrm{Z}
contains all dimension attributes in
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
. We call this condition the
aggregable property condition
.

(1)

Analytic filter
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Filter}_{T}(P\mid\mathrm{Y})
: By the condition
\mathrm{Y}\subseteq S_{D}
, all attributes in
\mathrm{Y}
are dimension attributes. By
\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}
we have
\mathrm{Y}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}
. Then for any non-empty partition
T^{x}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}=x}(T)
of
T
we can show that if
P
is true for some tuple in
T^{x}
, it is true for
all
tuples in
T^{x}
:
\mathrm{Z}
contains all attributes of filtering predicate
P
and we can show that for all
\mathrm{Z}=x
either
\mathrm{Z}=x\Rightarrow P(\mathrm{Y})
or
\mathrm{Z}=x\Rightarrow\neg P(\mathrm{Y})
. Therefore, the corresponding partition
T_{1}^{x}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}=x}(\mathpzc{Filter}_{T}(P\mid\mathrm{Y}))=\mathpzc{Filter}_{T}(P\wedge\mathrm{Z}=x\mid\mathrm{Z})
is either empty or equal to
T^{x}
.
Hence, for any
T^{x}
and attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
in
S
, we either have
\Pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T_{1}^{x})=\Pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T^{x})
(both partitions are identical with duplicates) or
T_{1}^{x}
is empty. Finally, the aggregable property condition holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}
.

(2)

Analytic projection
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Project}_{T}(\mathrm{Y},f(\mathrm{Z}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{M}})
: By definition of analytic projection, for any subset
\mathrm{X}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
, we have
\Pi_{\mathrm{X}}(T_{1})=\Pi_{\mathrm{X}}(T)
. Since
\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq S_{D}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}
, we have
\Pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T_{1})=\Pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T)
.
Finally, the aggregable property condition holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
.

(3)

Analytic aggregate
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Agg}_{T}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{G}}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}})\mid\mathrm{Y})
:
By definition of aggregate queries, for any
\mathrm{X}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
and partition
T^{x}
, we have
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(T_{1}^{x})=\pi_{\mathrm{X},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T^{x})
(duplicate eliminating projection). Since
\mathrm{Z}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}
, we then have
\pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T_{1}^{x})=\pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}}(T^{x})
. However,
T^{x}
generally contains several tuples that are merged into a single tuple in
T_{1}
. Therefore,
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
must be restricted to functions that are not sensitive to duplicates (
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT},\mathrm{MIN},\mathrm{MAX}\}
).
Finally, the aggregable property condition holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
.

(4)

Analytic pivot
T_{1}=\mathpzc{Pivot}_{T}(\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\mid\mathrm{Y})
: We apply similar arguments as for aggregate queries on the remaining attributes
\mathrm{Y}^{\prime}=S-\mathrm{Y}-\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}
in
T_{1}
. By definition of pivot queries, for any subset
\mathrm{X}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}^{\prime}
and partition
T^{x}
, we have
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(T_{1}^{x})=\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(T^{x})
(duplicate eliminating projection) and
\pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T_{1}^{x})=\pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T^{x})
in particular for
\mathrm{Z}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}^{\prime}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}^{\prime}
. However, in the general case,
T^{x}
contains several tuples that are merged into a single tuple in
T_{1}
and
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
must be restricted to functions that are not sensitive to duplicates (
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT},\mathrm{MIN},\mathrm{MAX}\}
).
Finally, the aggregable property condition holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{Y}-\mathrm{X}_{1}-\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}-\mathrm{Y}-\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}\}
.

Merge queries:

For merge queries
\mathpzc{Q}\,
over two tables
T
and
T^{\prime}
producing a table
T_{1}
, we also check the G-summarizability condition and the aggregable property condition as for unary queries:

•

For any couple of partitions
T^{x}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}=x}(T)
and
T_{1}^{x}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Z}=x}(T_{1})
, either at least one of the two partitions is empty or
\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T^{x})
is equal to
\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T_{1}^{x})
.

•

\mathrm{Z}
contains all dimension attributes in
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
.

(1)

Left-merge query
T(\textsc{S})\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}(\textsc{S}^{\prime})
:
By definition,
\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T)=\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T_{1})
(each tuple of
T
produces one or more tuples in
T_{1}
and vice versa). Thus, for any
\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}\subseteq\textsc{S}_{d}
we also have
\pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T)=\pi_{\mathrm{Z},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(T_{1})
. By definition, a tuple in
T
can only appear twice in
\Pi_{\mathrm{Z}}(T_{1})
if
\mathrm{Y}\not\mapsto\textsc{S}^{\prime}
. If that is the case,
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
must be restricted to functions that are not sensitive to duplicates:
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}\in\{\mathrm{COUNT\_DISTINCT},\mathrm{MIN},\mathrm{MAX}\}
.
Finally, the aggregable property condition holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}
.

(2)

Right-merge query
T(\textsc{S})\mathbin{\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}(\textsc{S}^{\prime})
or full-merge query
\mathpzc{Q}\,=T(\textsc{S})\mathbin{\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[-0.08612pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[7.0pt]{2.5pt}{0.4pt}}}_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}(\textsc{S}^{\prime})
:

(a)

\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Y}
: We assume that for all non-empty partitions
T^{\prime y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime})
of
T^{\prime}
, the corresponding partition
T^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T)
is either empty or
\pi_{Y}(T^{y})
is equal to
\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime}))
. From this assumption and the definition of right-outer join, it directly follows that for any non-empty partition
T_{1}^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T_{1})
of
T_{1}
and corresponding partition
T^{y}
,
T^{y}=\emptyset
or
\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T^{y})=\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T_{1}^{y})
. Then, for all
\mathrm{X}\supseteq\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, all non-empty partitions
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T))
are equal to
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T_{1}))
and since, by definition of
\mathrm{Z}
,
\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}\supseteq\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, the previous condition also holds for
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}
.
Therefore the G-summarizability condition holds on
T
and
T_{1}
for
\mathrm{Z}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
.
Finally, the aggregable property condition also holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}^{top}
.

(b)

\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\textsc{S}-\mathrm{Y}
: We assume that for all non-empty partitions,
T^{\prime y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime})
, and corresponding partitions
T^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T)
,
\pi_{Y}(T^{y})
is a subset of
\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime}))
. From this assumption and the definition of right-outer join, it directly follows that for any non-empty partition
T_{1}^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T_{1})
and corresponding partition
T^{y}
,
\pi_{\textsc{S}-\mathrm{Y}}(T_{1}^{y})-\pi_{\textsc{S}-\mathrm{Y}}(T^{y})
only contains
null
values. Then, for all
\mathrm{X}\subseteq\textsc{S}_{D}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\in\textsc{S}-\mathrm{Y}
, all non-empty partitions
\pi_{\mathrm{X},\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T))
are equal to
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x\wedge\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\neq null}(T_{1}))
and since, by definition of
\mathrm{Z}
,
\mathrm{Z}\subseteq\textsc{S}_{D}
, the previous condition also holds for
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}
. Therefore, the G-summarizability condition holds on
T
and
T_{1}
for
\mathrm{Z}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
.
Finally, the aggregable property condition also holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}
.

(c)

Otherwise: From the definition of right-merge and full-merge, it directly follows that for any non-empty partition
T_{1}^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Y}=y}(T_{1})
, the corresponding partition
T^{y}
is either empty or equal to
\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T_{1}^{y})
. Then, for all
\mathrm{X}\supseteq\mathrm{Y}
, all non-empty partitions
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T))
are equal to
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T_{1}))
and since, by definition of
\mathrm{Z}
,
\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}\supseteq\mathrm{Y}
, the previous condition also holds for
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}
. Therefore, the G-summarizability condition holds for
T
and
T_{1}
for
\mathrm{Z}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
.
Finally, the aggregable property condition also holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}
.

(3)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=T(\textsc{S})\bowtie_{\mathrm{Y}}T^{\prime}(\textsc{S}^{\prime})
:

(a)

"if" part: We assume that for all non-empty partitions
T^{\prime y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime})
, the corresponding partition
T^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T)
is contained in
\pi_{\mathrm{Y}}(T^{\prime y})
. From this assumption and the definition of inner join, it directly follows that any non-empty partition
T_{1}^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T_{1})
in the result is equal to the corresponding partition
T^{y}
:
\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T^{y})=\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T_{1}^{y})
. Then, for all
\mathrm{X}\supseteq\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, all non-empty partitions
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T))
are equal to
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T_{1}))
and since, by definition of
\mathrm{Z}
,
\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}\supseteq\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, the previous condition also holds for
X=\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}
. Therefore the G-summarizability condition holds on
T
and
T_{1}
for grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
and attribute
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
.
Finally, the aggregable property condition also holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}^{top}
.

(b)

"else" part: From the definition of inner join, it directly follows that for any non-empty partition
T_{1}^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{Y}=y}(T_{1})
, the corresponding partition
T^{y}
is equal to
\pi_{\textsc{S}}(T_{1}^{y})
. Then, for all
\mathrm{X}\supseteq\mathrm{Y}
, all non-empty partitions
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T_{1}))
are equal to
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T))
and since, by definition of
\mathrm{Z}
,
\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}\supseteq\mathrm{Y}
, the previous condition also holds for
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}
. Therefore the G-summarizability condition holds on
T
and
T_{1}
for groupîng set
\mathrm{Z}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
.
Finally, the aggregable property condition also holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}
.

Set queries:

In the following, let
\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}\subseteq S_{D}
denote the set of "highest" attributes in the set of dimension attributes
S_{D}\subseteq S
of
T
and
T^{\prime}
.

(1)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=T(\textsc{S})\cup T^{\prime}(\textsc{S})
: By the assumption
\pi_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}}(T)\cap\pi_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}}(T^{\prime})=\emptyset
and the definition of union, it follows that for any non-empty partition
T_{1}^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T_{1})
in the result, the corresponding partition
T^{y}
is either empty or
T^{y}
is equal to
T_{1}^{y}
. Then, for all
\mathrm{X}\supseteq\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, all non-empty partitions
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T))
are equal to
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T_{1}))
and since, by definition of
\mathrm{Z}
,
\mathrm{Z}\supseteq\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, the previous condition also holds for
\mathrm{Z}=\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
. Finally, the aggregable property condition also holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}^{top}
.

(2)

\mathpzc{Q}\,=T(\textsc{S})-T^{\prime}(\textsc{S})
: By assumption, all partitions
\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T)
are equal to or disjoint with
\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T^{\prime})
. Then, by the definition of set-difference, it follows that for any non-empty partition
T_{1}^{y}=\sigma_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}=y}(T_{1})
in the result, the corresponding partition
T^{y}
is either empty or
T^{y}
is equal to
T_{1}^{y}
. Then, for all
\mathrm{X}\supseteq\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, all non-empty partitions
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T))
are equal to
\pi_{\mathrm{X}}(\sigma_{\mathrm{X}=x}(T_{1}))
and since, by definition of
\mathrm{Z}
,
\mathrm{Z}\supseteq\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, the previous condition also holds for
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{Z}\cup\{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}\}
. Therefore, the G-summarizability condition holds on
T
and
T_{1}
for
\mathrm{Z}
and
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
.
Finally, the aggregable property condition also holds:
S_{r}\cap S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}=S_{D}-\mathrm{X}_{1}\cup\mathrm{Y}^{top}
.

∎

Observe that for merge queries and set queries we choose the "highest" dimension
\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
as candidates for checking the G-summarizability conditions. In fact, we might check this condition for any subset
\mathrm{Y}^{\prime}
of attributes from
\mathrm{Y}
or
S_{D}
instead of
\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
, and identify the minimal candidates for which these conditions hold. There are two main reasons for only choosing
\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
. First, checking the G-summarizability condition for a subset of attributes mainly corresponds to comparing the size of partitions in two different tables obtained by two aggregate queries. This basic operation is costly and the systematic exploration of all attribute subsets
\mathrm{Y}^{\prime}
might, even with efficient pruning techniques, take too much time in an interactive data exploration session. Secondly, the choice of the highest attributes
\mathrm{\mathrm{Y}}^{top}
is based on the realistic hypothesis that the majority of analytic queries aggregate values along these attributes and other lower attributes.

4.4.
Controlling G-summarizability using aggregable properties

The following proposition refines the propagation rules for aggregable properties of
Section

3.3
, using the results of
Proposition

4.15
, to guarantee the G-summarizability of attributes.

Proposition 0 (aggregable properties with G-summarizability for unary queries).

Let
\mathpzc{Q}\,
be a unary analytic query with some input table
T(S)
returning a table
T_{r}(\textsc{S}^{\prime})
, and
\textsc{S}_{D}
be all the dimension attributes of
S\cap\textsc{S}^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
be an attribute in
S\cap\textsc{S}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X})
holds in
T
.
Then, in the cases of queries
\mathpzc{Q}\,
of
Table

27
, the aggregable property
\mathbf{agg}_{\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}},\mathrm{X}^{\prime})
holds in
T_{r}
and is such that for all
\mathrm{Z}
where
\textsc{S}_{D}-\mathrm{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Z}
,
\mathrm{\scalebox{0.8}{A}}^{\prime}
is G-summarizable in
T
with respect to query
\mathpzc{Q}\,
, grouping set
\mathrm{Z}
, and function
\mathrm{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{F}}
.

.
